## 1. Authority and Non-Invention

### 1.1 Order of authority

1. Applicable External Binding Authority, but only when identified and interpreted by the named authorised human authority.
2. PROJECT_SPEC.md v1.0 FROZEN.
3. IMPLEMENTATION_BLUEPRINT.md v1.0 FROZEN (this document), as active Rank 2 target-HOW authority.
4. Approved ADRs.
5. Backlog or tracker.
6. Historical architecture, research, review, and repository evidence.
7. Agent notes.

QWEN_REPO_EVIDENCE.md and current code/tests are implementation evidence. K:/new/clinicopus2.md is historical HOW input. AGENTS.md is repository and engineering governance. None may override Product Spec behaviour.

### 1.2 Required conflict protocol

If a HOW choice would change behaviour, scope, permission, state meaning, error outcome, Trust Invariant, Global Story Contract, journey, or acceptance criterion, stop the affected design. Identify the Rank 1 IDs and record a Product-authority blocker. Do not select a lower-ranked answer. Blueprint-owned choices may be decided only when every permitted implementation preserves the same observable Rank 1 outcome.

AUTH, TEN, USR, APT, PAT, CAT, REP, AUD, BRN, OPS, OD-P*, OD-C*, OD-PH*, OD-L*, OD-R*, OD-T*, and OD-18..OD-22 remain exactly as Rank 1 records them. An interface can preserve a seam; an empty model, permissive default, or guessed transition cannot supply missing authority.

### 1.3 Verified source identity

The v0.2 edit computed all three required hashes locally. PROJECT_SPEC.md identifies KlinKlik, Version 1.0, Status FROZEN. All expected hashes match. The identity gate therefore passed.

### 1.4 Historical input policy

Historical HOW is used for compatible modular-monolith, PostgreSQL, REST, client, and operational context. Its IndexedDB clinical-draft, full Appointment lifecycle, ANC-specific lifecycle, Celery/Redis, object-storage, and outbox suggestions are not carried forward where Rank 1 or current evidence does not authorize or require them.

### 2.2 Direct repository inspection

The v0.2 pass inspected the real backend, frontend, test, CI, Docker, dependency, ADR, and migration files. Material current facts are:

- Django 5.2/DRF modular monolith with fourteen local Django apps: core, tenancy, accounts, audit, patients, scheduling, clinical, billing, maternity, laboratory, pharmacy, inventory, reporting, platformadmin.
- Next.js App Router with TanStack Query. Session and access token are held in memory; the refresh token is cookie-based; query cache clears at sign-out.
- PostgreSQL 16 is the target and local Compose database. SQLite is still the default settings/CI path.
- FORCE RLS policies and the audit immutability trigger exist only on PostgreSQL. The application role bootstrap intends NOSUPERUSER/NOBYPASSRLS.
- Visit is missing. QueueEntry, Encounter, TriageAssessment, basic invoice/payment/allocation, clinical versioning, audit, and idempotency are partial.
- laboratory, pharmacy, inventory, maternity, reporting, and platformadmin are app seams, not implemented workflow evidence.
- Current Payment has an invoice foreign key and current services allocate to one Invoice only.
- Current idempotency checks and persists outside the domain transaction, scopes uniqueness to organisation plus key, and can race on first use.
- CI runs backend migrations/tests without a PostgreSQL service and does not run Playwright. SQLite cannot prove RLS, row locks, or PostgreSQL constraints.
- Redis is present in Docker Compose but no Redis client or application use was found. It is not evidence that Redis is required.

### 2.3 Evidence classifications

| Classification | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| ALIGNED | Current implementation can be retained subject to proof and cleanup |
| PARTIAL | Useful structure exists but does not satisfy the full Rank 1 contract |
| CONFLICT | Current behaviour or state differs from Rank 1 and must migrate |
| MISSING | No substantive implementation exists |
| BLOCKED | Rank 1 authority or a required Blueprint approval is absent |

### 3.3 Additional v0.1 architecture corrections

v0.2 also corrects eight architecture defects not represented as the original twelve flags:

1. A proposed fifteenth visits app violated the governed fourteen-app boundary.
2. A nonexistent prescribing app caused double ownership with pharmacy.
3. core.workflow inverted the declared dependency direction.
4. Direct domain-to-domain calls created cycles and unclear transaction ownership.
5. The idempotency response was saved outside the business transaction and first-use races were unresolved.
6. Legacy migration invented MIGRATED states/reasons instead of quarantining ambiguous records.
7. Outbox, Redis, object storage, report jobs, and clinical-rule tables were prematurely required.
8. Client facility switching did not fully isolate late responses and cached PHI by authority epoch.

## 4. Architecture Principles

| ID | Principle | Implementation consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| BP-01 | Product Spec meaning is invariant under implementation choice | Mechanism changes require equal observable-outcome proof |
| BP-02 | Evolve the existing modular monolith | No microservices, Kubernetes, GraphQL, serverless fragmentation, CQRS/event sourcing, service mesh, or general event bus |
| BP-03 | Server is the only authority | Every protected decision is recomputed from authoritative server state |
| BP-04 | Default deny at every boundary | Missing/uncertain scope, authority, configuration, credential, or state denies safely |
| BP-05 | Domain rules live in explicit services | Views authorize/orchestrate/serialize; serializers validate shape; models persist and enforce local invariants |
| BP-06 | One transition owner per aggregate | No state transition from serializers, model save hooks, signals, or frontend code |
| BP-07 | History is append-correct | Clinical, lab, stock, final financial, payment, receipt, and audit history is corrected by a supplied follow-on record/path |
| BP-08 | Use the simplest sufficient concurrency control | Prefer constraints/conditional writes; add row locks or versions only where contention requires |
| BP-09 | One committed high-impact outcome | One application command owns the complete transaction and safe losing result |
| BP-10 | High-impact commands are retry-safe | Operation-scoped request identity, fingerprint, and replayed committed result |
| BP-11 | Mandatory audit is transactional | A required audit write failure rolls back the protected action |
| BP-12 | Minimise sensitive payload | No PHI in generic logs, telemetry, diagnostics, analytics, error bodies, or audit payloads |
| BP-13 | Server/database time is authoritative | Store UTC; apply the fixed Africa/Kampala facility-day boundary |
| BP-14 | Unresolved product authority is a blocker, not a default | Provide a narrow interface or refusal; do not create behaviourless domain records |
| BP-15 | No high-impact offline completion | Offline UI may preserve a non-PHI attempt where allowed, never claim completion |
| BP-16 | PostgreSQL proves PostgreSQL properties | SQLite results cannot satisfy RLS, lock, partial-unique, trigger, or race gates |
| BP-17 | Seams are free; stubs are not | Keep package/API boundaries, but add schema only for an authorized, tested slice |
| BP-18 | Infrastructure requires a named need | Redis, brokers, outbox, object storage, and tracing are absent until justified and authorized |
| BP-19 | Cross-domain orchestration belongs above domains | A model-free application package owns multi-domain commands; domains never import it |
| BP-20 | Design readiness is not implementation authorization | Slice status uses separate design and authorization columns |

---

## 5. Target Topology and Deployment Boundary

KlinKlik remains one deployable modular Django/DRF monolith, one Next.js client, and one PostgreSQL database. The backend may run multiple stateless web processes for capacity, but remains one application and schema. A scheduler invocation may run the same release binary for an explicitly authorized sweep; it is not a new service boundary.

Baseline V1 does not require Redis, Celery, a broker, an outbox, object storage, a data warehouse, or a separate reporting database. Their presence in historical HOW or local Compose is not a requirement. No provider, region, processor, or production topology is selected while OD-L1/OD-L2 remain unresolved.

### 5.1 Logical layers

Client -> DRF views -> application.commands -> domain services/queries -> Django models -> PostgreSQL

Cross-cutting primitives flow inward only:

- core: tenant transaction, idempotency, canonical technical errors, authoritative clock, version helpers.
- audit: append-only audit writer called by the application command.
- application: model-free command orchestration; imports core, audit, and domain public interfaces.
- domain apps: own aggregates and transitions; never import application and never mutate another app's model.

### 5.2 WHAT/HOW boundary

Rank 1 remains the only source for states, guards, roles, errors, scope, journeys, blockers, and acceptance outcomes. This Blueprint owns package placement, persistence representation, command boundaries, lock/constraint choices, staged migration, client composition, test proof, and deployment mechanics only.

Frontend feature names do not create backend ownership. A prescribing UI feature calls pharmacy-owned Prescription APIs. A payments UI feature calls billing-owned Payment APIs.

### 6.1 Entity ownership invariant

Every persistent entity has exactly one owning app. Foreign keys may identify records in another owner, but only the owner mutates its aggregate. Cross-owner changes occur through one application command calling each owner's public transition service.

---

## 7. Dependency Direction and Import Enforcement

### 7.1 Permitted direction

1. core has no domain imports.
2. tenancy and accounts use core.
3. domain apps use core and stable identity references, but mutations remain local.
4. audit exposes a writer; domains do not query audit to decide product outcomes.
5. application imports public domain services/queries and audit.
6. views import application commands for mutations and owner queries for reads.
7. frontend imports generated API contracts and feature-local adapters.

Domain services must not call another domain's mutation service, write another app's model, import application, or use Django signals for orchestration. Read-only cross-domain needs use a named public query returning an immutable projection. Query interfaces cannot cause writes, acquire workflow locks, or decide transitions for the caller. Inventory may hold a database reference to the pharmacy-owned stable MedicationProduct identity and therefore depends one way on pharmacy migrations/types; pharmacy never imports inventory. `application.pharmacy` queries inventory availability and passes the immutable projection into pharmacy owner services, preserving an acyclic runtime and migration graph.

### 7.2 Enforcement

CI adds an import-boundary test that parses backend imports and rejects:

- any core import from a domain or application package;
- any domain import from application;
- any direct import of another domain's models from a services module for mutation;
- views importing domain models for a protected mutation;
- serializers or model hooks invoking transition services;
- signals performing workflow, stock, money, clinical finalization, or cross-domain work.

Temporary legacy exceptions are enumerated by exact import and removed in their migration slice; no wildcard allow-list is permitted.

---

## 8. Application Command Layer

The target package is backend/application/. It is not in INSTALLED_APPS, has no models, and owns no domain state. Commands are explicit functions or small command objects grouped by workflow. Every mutation endpoint calls exactly one application command.

Declared model-free subpackages are `application.reception`, `application.queue`, `application.clinical`, `application.laboratory`, `application.pharmacy`, `application.inventory`, `application.billing`, `application.maternity`, `application.operations`, `application.catalogue`, and `application.documents`. `application.documents` coordinates owner queries, deterministic `reporting.renderers`, owner-held print/reprint counters where supplied, and the audit writer; it owns neither persistent documents nor report definitions and cannot bypass REP/AUD authority.

### 8.1 Command contract

Each command declares:

- canonical operation name and Rank 1 IDs;
- actor, organisation, facility, capability, and object-scope inputs;
- idempotency requirement and request fingerprint fields;
- records to resolve and deterministic lock plan;
- one outer tenant transaction owner;
- domain transitions invoked in order;
- safe audit facts, with no raw PHI;
- committed response reference and canonical error mapping;
- stale/losing result;
- tests, including retries and competing requests.

Application commands may coordinate owner services; they do not contain clinical interpretation, pricing policy, permission invention, or state-machine rules. Owner services validate their own Rank 1 guards and return transition results plus audit facts. The command ensures the combined CMC outcome is atomic.

### 8.2 Command-family navigation

This table is navigation only. The complete normative command contracts are CMD-001..CMD-136 in §32; CMD-097 and CMD-107 are retired tombstones.
