### 8.3 Query and mutation separation

A command may pre-resolve identifiers through public queries before locking, but it must re-read and validate all authoritative mutable data inside the transaction. Query projections are never sufficient authority for a protected transition. Multi-domain reads for UI composition may be assembled by an application query service without changing ownership.

### 8.4 Normative read/query-contract register

These reusable read contracts are model-free and non-mutating. A query contract
may compose immutable projections from multiple owning domains, but it cannot
write, acquire workflow locks, decide a transition, grant authority, or be used
as a substitute for command-side revalidation.

| Query ID | Contract / owner | Source owner queries and scope | Permission, payload, freshness and tests |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| QRY-001 | Attendance list / application.reception.attendance_query | Composes scheduling Visit/QueueEntry with clinical, laboratory, pharmacy and billing read projections; facility-scoped Africa/Kampala day and organisation context | visit.read_list; receptionist receives stage, wait time, assigned staff and payment status but no diagnosis, complaint, vitals or medication payload; no PHI_READ for the list; 20-second refresh and stale banner; tests cover stage transitions, >45-minute threshold, 300-row load, payload minimisation, facility isolation and late-response discard |
| QRY-002 | Protected Visit read / application.reception.visit_query | Scheduling Visit owner query with tenancy/accounts scope checks; facility B cannot discover facility A unless supplied same-organisation BRN read authority exists | Default-deny and 404 existence hiding; cross-organisation access never allowed; RLS and service scope tests cover the denied and explicitly authorised cases |
| QRY-003 | Encounter composed context / application.clinical.encounter_context | Composes clinical Encounter/note plus patient, allergy, visit history, laboratory order/result, prescription and dispense projections from their owning queries | Role-filtered minimum necessary payload, one PHI_READ/access-audit fact, no SSR or persistent PHI cache; tests cover owner composition, facility scope, role filtering, empty states and audit cardinality |
| QRY-004 | Queue priority projection / scheduling.queries.queue_priority_projection | Read-only QueueEntry/Visit projection for QUE-008-AC04 in facility scope | Server clock evaluates the supplied >10-minute emergency visual escalation; no mutation or automatic scoring; tests cover ordering, threshold, stale/late response and facility isolation |
| QRY-005 | Queue, location and operational worklists / application.queue.operational_queries | Composes scheduling-owned QueueEntry/Visit projections, PC-081 board/no-response display policy and only the explicitly named immutable owner projections needed by QUE-002/009..012/014/015 | Facility-scoped, role-minimal payloads; server-derived wait/threshold values; public board refuses activation until its exact identifier mode is configured; no clinical payload beyond each supplied row contract; tests cover scope hiding, ordering, empty/overdue/follow-up states, 15-second refresh, late-response isolation and pagination |
| QRY-006 | Triage, vitals, procedure and clinician worklists / application.clinical.triage_queries | Composes clinical TriageRecord/VitalsObservation/Encounter/ProcedureOrder heads with scheduling location, PC-081 supplied quick-pick/checklist options and only the supplied history references | Clinical capability and facility scope are enforced per projection; options insert editable human text and empty complaint configuration permits the supplied free-text fallback; pending/performed procedure tasks and the supplied unresolved-task view are derived from PC-054 without inventing a broader procedure lifecycle; no automatic acuity/priority/interaction interpretation; auditable history reads use AUDITED-READ-01; tests cover role payload differences, procedure task visibility, amendment refresh, five-most-recent ordering and BRN fail-closed behavior |
| QRY-007 | Laboratory order, worklist, result and supervisor projections / application.laboratory.lab_queries | Composes laboratory-owned catalogue/order/item/custody/result/analyte projections with scheduling collection-queue references and billing gate summaries | Unreleased values are excluded; patient-facing collection and bench lifecycle remain distinct; server time drives age/turnaround; auditable result/report reads use AUDITED-READ-01; tests cover partial release, SAMPLE_REJECTED, range snapshots, empty/pagination/performance and facility isolation |
| QRY-008 | Pharmacy, prescription, dispense and inventory projections / application.pharmacy.queries, composing inventory.queries read-only projections | Composes pharmacy-owned MedicationProduct/Prescription/Dispense/PharmacyClaim with inventory-owned immutable availability/ledger projections and the exact PC-081 uncollected-window, expiry-horizon, blind-count and counselling display policy; neither owner calls the other's mutation service | Defaults are exactly 7 days, 90 days and blind count=true; expired/non-usable stock is excluded, controlled/Class A refused, and no allergy/interaction interpretation occurs; tests cover EAT date boundaries, FEFO display, claim visibility, batch/dispense history, stock-count views, scope and PHI minimisation |
| QRY-009 | Billing, payment, receipt and cashier projections / application.billing.finance_queries | Composes billing-owned Invoice/line-version, Payment/Allocation, Receipt, CreditNote, Refund and CashierShift projections plus minimum scheduling location context and exact PC-081 shift/variance policy | All amounts use the sole authoritative billing projection; stale-shift/variance alerts remain unavailable when their no-default policy value is unset and never invent an external notification; role-filtered descriptions and one-Payment/one-Receipt identity; tests cover multi-Invoice arithmetic, reversal/credit/refund history, shift thresholds, empty/export projections, scope and concurrency refresh |
| QRY-010 | ANC projections / application.maternity.queries, composing reporting.queries only where separately authorised | Composes maternity-owned ANC records with standard clinical/lab projections; reporting-shaped projections remain unavailable where REP, APT, clinical or external-authority blockers apply | No ANC lifecycle, protocol, risk score, interpretation, appointment or report authority is invented; tests distinguish supplied negative/default behavior from blocked positive branches and preserve exact blocker IDs |
| QRY-011 | Authorised document and export projection / application.documents.render_query | Reads an immutable or version-pinned owner projection and passes it to deterministic reporting.renderers; it owns no Product record or report definition | Authority is checked before content composition; the response is PHI-minimal for the actor and bound to source versions/content hash. Required print/export/access evidence uses AUDITED-READ-01. Receipt and Visit-slip counters remain CMD-081 and CMD-012; RCP-005 remains client-local and model-free |
| QRY-012 | Management and regulatory-shaped reporting projections / application.reporting.queries | Composes only owner-supplied projections for a specifically authorised REP/AUD surface; it owns no source record and does not infer missing catalogue, fiscal, legal, clinical or submission authority | Every blocked REP/CAT/AUD dependency fails closed with its exact BP-BL IDs; exports compose through QRY-011 and AUDITED-READ-01 only after the reporting projection is authorised; no certified submission, automatic interpretation or external delivery is implied |

AUDITED-READ-01 is the only read-with-required-evidence protocol. The API coordinator opens one short tenant_atomic transaction, resolves authority, executes exactly one named QRY contract, writes the single required PHI_READ/print/export AuditEvent from a predeclared redacted fact, and returns the projection only after that audit commits. The QRY implementation remains pure and cannot write. Audit failure returns no protected payload and a non-sensitive retryable technical failure. One composed view writes one access fact, never one per owner subquery. For RCP-003 print/reprint, the coordinator locks the exact printable source head in LOCK-01 order, obtains the next per-document copy_number from existing PC-047 print facts, inserts the fact under the PC-047 uniqueness guard, and returns the deterministic version-pinned render only after commit; concurrent prints therefore receive distinct increasing numbers. Receipt and Visit-slip owner counters remain CMD-081 and CMD-012 and use that committed owner count as the PC-047 copy number. Every repeated print request is a new access unless an exact owner command supplies idempotent reprint semantics; no print repeats the underlying clinical or financial action.

Traceability rows use these IDs in blueprint_section and retain application_command_id=N/A for query-only or AUDITED-READ-01 acceptance criteria. An AuditEvent written by AUDITED-READ-01 does not turn the owner query into a domain mutation or permit a fake command mapping.

---

## 9. Transaction Ownership, Locking, and Concurrency

### 9.1 Product-action transaction rule

Every product action has one outer application command. It opens exactly one core.services.tenant_atomic transaction with the verified organisation ID. On PostgreSQL this sets LOCAL app.current_org_id before tenant-owned reads or writes. The same transaction owns idempotency claim/completion, all participating domain transitions, mandatory successful-action audit, and the committed result.

A multi-action journey may have sequential committed commands only where Rank 1 explicitly requires a durable intermediate outcome. REC-002 is the named V1 case: CMD-002 commits Patient registration, then the client invokes CMD-001 with its own operation identity. A failed check-in never rolls back or duplicates the committed Patient. This rule is not a general saga escape hatch; no command may split one Rank-1 atomic product outcome merely for convenience.

A refused action normally commits no domain or success-audit mutation. Where Rank 1 explicitly requires durable denial evidence, the business transaction rolls back first and the denial evidence follows the separate §17.1 denial-audit protocol. That audit transaction cannot turn refusal into success or commit part of the refused domain outcome.

Domain mutation services are transaction-neutral in the target: they do not open independent durable transactions and must assert that a transaction is active. Existing transaction.atomic decorators are removed slice by slice only after the calling endpoint moves to an application command. Nested savepoints are allowed solely for recoverable database-control flow such as a unique-claim race; they do not create a second product outcome.

Single-domain mutations still enter through an application command. This keeps authorization, idempotency, audit, and error behavior uniform without making the domain transition complex.

### 9.2 Deterministic lock order

Commands acquire only required existing records and use the following normative order, LOCK-01. Rows within a type are ordered by stable UUID unless a contract names an immutable sequence key. A command that has acquired a later rank must not subsequently acquire an earlier rank. New rows are not pretend-locked: their exactly-once source constraint, parent lock, or allocator row is named in the command contract.

1. Configuration/catalogue/identity row, in suborder Facility, FacilityWorkflowPolicy, Department, ServiceDefinition, PriceList, ServicePrice, MedicationProduct, ProductPrice, LabTestDefinition, LabAnalyteDefinition, LabReferenceRange, StockLocation, Supplier, Batch, PaymentMethodConfiguration, InventoryThreshold
2. Patient
3. PatientAllergy, PatientAllergyState, PatientProblem, or PatientObstetricSummary
4. ArrivalEnquiry
5. Visit
6. QueueEntry
7. Appointment fragment
8. ANCEnrolment
9. ANCVisit
10. Encounter
11. ClinicalNote, TriageRecord, VitalsObservation, or other Encounter/Visit clinical head
12. LabOrder
13. LabOrderItem
14. LabSpecimenCustody
15. LabResult, then LateResultReview
16. Prescription
17. PharmacyClaim
18. Dispense
19. StockReceipt, StockCount, StockAdjustment, QuarantineRecord, or DisposalRecord parent
20. CashierShift
21. RefundRequest
22. Payment
23. Invoice
24. Receipt
25. BatchLocationBalance
26. NumberSequence or other explicit allocator row

The command may query identifiers without locks first, then locks and revalidates the full set in order. A changing relationship causes VERSION_CONFLICT/retry, never an out-of-order opportunistic lock. A mutable child governed by `PARENT_AFTER_PARENT` in §31.4 is locked immediately after its named parent and before the command resumes the global order; siblings use stable UUID/source-line order. Append-only children are inserted after parent locks and are not later mutated. New-row uniqueness waits do not count as a later lock acquisition. The exhaustive PC disposition in §31.4 is part of LOCK-01; an unclassified new PC fails publication.

### 9.3 Concurrency technique by aggregate

| Record / operation | Primary technique | Database guard | Safe loser behavior |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Visit create | Idempotency + conditional/partial uniqueness | one same-facility local-day non-terminal Visit per patient, subject only to supplied exception | return authorized existing reference or ALREADY_EXISTS |
| Queue transition/call | row lock + current-state predicate | active work-path and IN_SERVICE uniqueness required by SM-01 | current state / ALREADY_COMPLETED / VERSION_CONFLICT |
| Queue priority/repeat task | QueueEntry row lock + operation idempotency | one active priority outcome per version; one repeat request per source identity | current priority/task or VERSION_CONFLICT |
| TriageRecord current version | optimistic version/ETag; row lock on completion | one current record per Visit; append versions | current version and reconciliation required |
| Encounter create/resume/sign | Visit then Encounter lock; note version ETag | one non-terminal Encounter for supplied Visit path; unique note versions | same Encounter/current state |
| ClinicalNote mutation | Encounter then note lock + If-Match | signed content immutable; version uniqueness | VERSION_CONFLICT with authorized current ETag |
| LabOrderItem transition | parent/readiness-set locks in order | legal current-state conditional transition; unique result versions | current item/readiness state |
| Prescription activation | Encounter then Prescription lock | source-event and prescription identity uniqueness | existing prescription outcome |
| Dispense handover | Dispense, applicable Invoice, ordered balances | one committed handover; movement source uniqueness; balance check | no stock effect; committed current outcome |
| Stock movement | ordered BatchLocationBalance locks | unique source identity; check resulting usable balance nonnegative | refuse entire bundle |
| Invoice line source | Invoice lock + idempotency | unique source-event identity per Invoice | reuse line/current Invoice |
| Payment confirm | ordered Invoice locks + idempotency | allocations cannot exceed Payment or permitted due; unique operation identity | replay Payment/Receipt |
| Payment reverse | Queue dependencies, Payment, then affected Invoices | one reversal reference; all effective allocations reversed as one outcome | current reversal/current invoices |
| CreditNote / Refund | Invoice plus referenced Payment as required | remaining creditable/refundable bounds and source uniqueness | no double credit/refund |
| PatientProblem / PatientObstetricSummary | Patient then patient-level row | one current version/head per stable identity; append history | current version / VERSION_CONFLICT |
| ANC enrolment/contact | Patient, ANCEnrolment, then ANCVisit | one active enrolment; unique contact sequence per enrolment | existing active/contact or VERSION_CONFLICT |
| Pharmacy claim | Prescription then unique active claim | partial UNIQUE(prescription) WHERE released_at IS NULL | 409 with authorised claimant identity |
| Stock-count approval/post | StockCount/StockAdjustment then ordered balances | distinct authorised approver; movement source uniqueness | current pending/posted outcome |

No negative usable stock, over-allocation, duplicate consultation charge, multiple active Encounter, multiple winner, or partial high-impact bundle may be visible after a failed or losing transaction. These rules implement TI-09, TI-10, TI-11, and CMC-17; they do not replace Rank 1 transition guards.

### 9.4 Stale writes

Mutable APIs expose a version or strong ETag and require If-Match where a stale edit could overwrite newer state. Missing precondition uses the Rank 1 canonical error family. A conflict response contains only the authorized current reference/state/version and recovery action; it never leaks hidden record existence or PHI. State commands also recheck their from-state under lock even when an ETag was supplied.

### 9.5 PostgreSQL isolation and database retry

The target default transaction isolation is PostgreSQL `READ COMMITTED`, matching the inspected Django/psycopg configuration and the row-lock/unique-constraint algorithms above. No command in this Blueprint requires a stronger isolation level. A future command-specific `REPEATABLE READ` or `SERIALIZABLE` choice requires a Blueprint change with an anomaly proof and cannot be selected ad hoc in a service.

Every authoritative predicate is re-read after the required row locks; projection recomputation uses the locked rows, and uniqueness constraints arbitrate absent-row races. The application transaction wrapper recognizes PostgreSQL serialization failure `40001` and deadlock `40P01`, rolls back the whole attempt, and retries the complete command at most three times with the same operation, idempotency key and fingerprint. It never retries a validation/permission/state error, never changes lock order, and never exposes an intermediate attempt. After the bounded database retry is exhausted, the API returns a retryable service failure/UNKNOWN reconciliation instruction; the client reuses the same key. PG-G5, PG-G9 and PG-G10 prove this behavior against PostgreSQL.

---

## 10. Idempotency Protocol

### 10.1 Scope and record

Target uniqueness is organisation_id + canonical operation + key. Facility, actor authority epoch, target identifiers, method, canonical path identity, and normalized material payload participate in a SHA-256 fingerprint. The raw secret/token is not stored. The stored result contains safe record references, status, response schema version, completion time, and the exact committed response needed for replay; it contains no unnecessary PHI.

### 10.2 First-use algorithm

1. View extracts Idempotency-Key and validates syntax; it does not save a response.
2. Application command enters tenant_atomic and computes the operation fingerprint.
3. Inside a savepoint, insert the unique IdempotencyRecord.
4. If insert wins, run the command, write mandatory audit, store the safe response on that record, and commit once.
5. If uniqueness loses, roll back only the savepoint, lock/read the winner after PostgreSQL resolves the unique wait, and compare fingerprint.
6. Same fingerprint returns the committed stored result. Different fingerprint returns the canonical idempotency conflict. If the winning transaction rolled back, the losing request may retry the claim.

The bounded database retry in §9.5 wraps steps 2–6. Each failed database attempt leaves no committed idempotency row; a retry re-enters with the identical operation identity and cannot create an additional Product outcome.

No separately committed IN_PROGRESS row is required. A crash before commit rolls back both claim and effects. A lost HTTP response after commit is recovered by replay. This removes the current check-then-act race and the current post-transaction save_response gap.

### 10.3 Failure and unknown outcome

Validation or authorization failures create no durable high-impact outcome unless Rank 1 explicitly requires a denial audit. Database failure rolls back claim, domain effects, and mandatory audit together. The client treats timeout/disconnect as UNKNOWN, queries the operation result or retries with the same key, and never changes the key to force a second attempt. A materially changed intent must use a new key only after explicit user reconciliation.

OD-T2 retention duration remains OPEN. Until resolved, money/stock/clinical-finalization idempotency records are not purged by a production job. The Blueprint does not declare an exact retention period.

---

## 11. Tenancy, Identity, Permission, and RLS

### 11.1 Scope model

Organisation is the tenant root. Facility is a branch, never a tenant. Every tenant-owned record carries organisation context; facility-owned records also carry facility context. Foreign-key consistency must prevent an organisation/facility mismatch even if an application query is wrong.

Every request resolves authoritative organisation membership, facility grant, department/object scope, capability, credential when supplied, and revocation state on the server. A client-selected facility is only a requested context. Cross-organisation access always fails closed. Cross-facility access fails closed unless an explicitly supplied and currently authorized BRN capability applies; no BRN exception exists by default.

AUTH/TEN/USR absence remains bounded: missing or uncertain authority denies protected action. This Blueprint does not invent invitation, reset, MFA, grant administration, professional credential, token lifetime, or revocation propagation behavior.

### 11.2 Tenant transaction and background work

No tenant model query may occur before tenant_atomic establishes app.current_org_id on PostgreSQL. Authentication bootstrap that must identify a tenant uses the separately reviewed bootstrap path and then enters tenant context before tenant-owned access. Background/scheduled commands require an explicit, validated organisation ID per invocation; no global unscoped ORM loop is permitted under the app role.

### 11.3 RLS target

All tenant-owned tables have ENABLE RLS, FORCE RLS, and matching USING/WITH CHECK policy. Production uses a non-owner, NOSUPERUSER, NOBYPASSRLS application role. A missing app.current_org_id must fail closed rather than return all rows. Migrations run under a separate migration role and cannot be the web role. New tenant tables fail CI if policy coverage is absent.

Current dynamic migration coverage is not sufficient proof for tables added after the migration ran; every new table migration must explicitly apply/verify policy or use a tested migration helper. Runtime startup/pre-deploy verification enumerates all organisation_id tables and fails on missing policy, FORCE flag, or unsafe role attributes.

---

## 12. State-Machine Ownership

Rank 1 contains the transitions, guards, roles, errors, audit outcomes, and exceptional paths. This section defines only implementation ownership and proof. Implementations import canonical state constants from their owner module and tests compare them to a checked trace fixture derived from Rank 1; no generic core.transition function contains domain states.

| SM | Owner app / aggregate | Canonical terminal summary | Transition service | Command transaction / concurrency | Migration treatment |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SM-01 | scheduling / QueueEntry | COMPLETED, TRANSFERRED, CANCELLED, LWBS, EXPIRED | scheduling.services.queue | application command; row lock + active uniqueness | reconcile current noncanonical states before contract |
| SM-02 | scheduling / Visit | CLOSED, CANCELLED_ERROR | scheduling.services.visit | Visit-first lock; same-day open uniqueness | new nullable links, deterministic backfill, manual exceptions |
| SM-03 | clinical / TriageRecord | COMPLETED current version | clinical.services.triage | version/ETag; append corrections | rename TriageAssessment; no invented acuity logic |
| SM-04 | clinical / Encounter | SIGNED with ENC-019 exception, VOIDED | clinical.services.encounter | Visit/Queue/Encounter/note lock; idempotent sign | state reconciliation; ambiguous CLOSED records quarantined |
| SM-05 | laboratory / LabOrderItem | RELEASED, CANCELLED | laboratory.services.items | item lock; application readiness recomputation | new implementation; no IN_PROGRESS state while OD-10 open |
| SM-06 | pharmacy / Prescription | DISPENSED, PARTIALLY_DISPENSED_CLOSED, NOT_DISPENSED, CANCELLED | pharmacy.services.prescription | Encounter then Prescription; source identity | new implementation; no separate prescribing app |
| SM-07 | pharmacy / Dispense | CANCELLED, DISPENSED, REVERSED | pharmacy.services.dispense | Dispense/finance gate/balance locks | new implementation |
| SM-08 | billing / Invoice | VOIDED; paid state recomputable only by supplied events | billing.services.invoice | Payment/Invoice locks as command requires | VOID->VOIDED; projection backfill/verify |
| SM-09 | billing / Payment | REVERSED | billing.services.payment | Payment then affected Invoice locks | POSTED->CONFIRMED; decouple invoice FK |
| SM-10 | scheduling / Appointment fragment | UNSUPPLIED/NONE | no general lifecycle service | only supplied fragment may be commanded | existing broader states are not target authority; APT blocked |
| SM-11 | maternity / ANCEnrolment fragment | UNSUPPLIED/NONE | composition service only | delegates to standard application commands | no ANC-specific contact/lab/prescription state machine |

### 12.1 Cross-machine ownership

CMC-01..CMC-17 are implemented by application commands, never by hidden model hooks or domain-to-domain calls. The application command owns atomic agreement; each domain owner validates its transition. Required cross-read examples include:

- CMC-01/02/03/04/05: same Encounter, distinct Encounter and Queue readiness, all-blocking lab condition, collection queue distinct from bench lifecycle, ENC-018/ENC-019 cleanup.
- CMC-06/07: exact-one consultation charge, ordinary and TRI-013 Visit transitions, PAY_BEFORE_TRIAGE, and no emergency triage QueueEntry invention.
- CMC-08/09/10: lab/pharmacy payment gate presence, same provisional Dispense, and undelivered-only reversal effects.
- CMC-11/12/13: signing activates prescriptions, handover/stock agreement, expired/controlled refusal.
- CMC-14/15/16/17: allocation and invoice truth, safe Visit closure, time/authority boundaries, and one atomic high-impact outcome.

---

## 13. Visit, Queue, Triage, and Encounter Reconciliation

### 13.1 Visit placement and links

Visit is added to scheduling, not a new Django app. It is the attendance/workflow parent required by SM-02. QueueEntry belongs to Visit. Encounter, TriageRecord, LabOrder, Prescription, and Visit-linked Invoice carry a protected Visit reference consistent with organisation, facility, and patient. Standalone paths such as explicitly authorized LAB_ONLY or retail still use the Rank 1 Visit/Invoice rules; this Blueprint does not invent a bypass.

The target database constraint enforces the supplied same-facility local-day open-Visit rule. Any authorized second-episode exception must be represented by an explicit Rank 1 authority reference and tested; absence fails with VISIT_ALREADY_OPEN.

### 13.2 Ordinary check-in

ordinary_check_in locks or creates the Visit, validates patient/destination/pricing/authority, creates the applicable initial QueueEntry, creates or reuses one Visit Invoice, and inserts exactly one consultation source line. The whole bundle is one transaction and one idempotency operation. A missing price or unsafe destination commits none of the ordinary bundle. The Invoice is ISSUED after successful chargeable check-in exactly as Rank 1 requires.

PAY_BEFORE_TRIAGE creates the same initial path but leaves the triage QueueEntry WAITING_PAYMENT and absent from the actionable triage worklist. Payment release changes that same entry to WAITING. Pre-service reversal may re-gate only the supplied undelivered states; it never creates a new Visit, QueueEntry, or charge.

### 13.3 TRI-013 emergency start

emergency_visit_start creates/reuses one Visit OPEN, begins the human emergency TriageRecord, moves that same Visit to IN_PROGRESS, and creates the supplied consultation QueueEntry without inventing a triage QueueEntry. Registration/identity/financial setup remains explicit and closure-blocking. emergency_setup_complete later links the surviving Patient and creates/reuses the exact-one consultation charge and Invoice on that original Visit. It cannot repeat the Visit transition or generalize the care-first exception.

The supplied TRI-013 patient-merge correction is its only currently authorized merge behavior. Field conflicts require the Rank 1 explicit resolution; wider PAT merge remains blocked.

### 13.4 Laboratory loop and signing

One active Encounter belongs to the Visit. Ordering/parking uses that Encounter. Encounter AWAITING_RESULTS and consultation QueueEntry ON_HOLD are distinct. All blocking items RELEASED or CANCELLED causes Encounter RESULTS_READY and QueueEntry READY_TO_RESUME atomically. Partial release and SAMPLE_REJECTED do not qualify.

Normal resume and manual early resume both reuse the same Encounter and existing consultation QueueEntry. ENC-018 signs directly from the pending path, sets signed_with_pending_orders, and completes the held entry with the supplied reason. It does not fake a resume through OPEN and never later becomes READY_TO_RESUME. CLOSED(PENDING_RESULTS) is a Visit closure reason/path, not a reopened Encounter state. LAB-023 late results preserve the signed/closed state and use the supplied addendum/review path.

SIGNED clinical content is immutable. ENC-019 is the only supplied exceptional SIGNED to VOIDED Encounter correction and must first resolve actionable lab and prescription dependencies exactly as CMC-05/11 require. Released results and handed-over stock remain historical and use their own correction paths.

### 13.5 Closure and stale work

visit_close locks Visit first, queries and then locks required blockers in canonical order, and either commits one allowed closure outcome or returns the exact authorized blockers. PARTIALLY_PAID, pending emergency financial setup, actionable QueueEntry/lab/prescription/dispense, and incomplete clinical state follow Rank 1 REC-012; no Blueprint shortcut applies.

REC-005 stale abandonment is implemented by CMD-129 only after the applicable REC-012 unresolved-work safety guards pass. Its explicit supplied cleanup is part of the same abandonment outcome: stale QueueEntries are terminalised, an applicable unpaid Invoice is voided through BIL-004, an applicable PAID Invoice is preserved, and the new ordinary Visit path is invoked by CMD-001 only after the stale Visit closure commits. PARTIALLY_PAID remains a refusal with durable denial evidence where Rank 1 requires it. CMD-130 is the separately authorised same-day second-episode override and does not close or rewrite the original Visit.

OD-22 remains OPEN/BLOCKED. The only current bounded automatic handling is the Rank 1 safe default for zero-record stale Visits; records-bearing stale Visits are flagged for human review. No scheduler silently closes them.

### 13.6 Safe legacy migration

Migration phases are expand, inventory, deterministic backfill, exception reconciliation, dual-read, new-write cutover, constraint activation, and contract.

- Add nullable Visit and new canonical-state fields without rewriting existing values.
- Build a durable migration reconciliation inventory containing technical record IDs, detected evidence, proposed mapping class, status, and reviewer reference; no PHI payload copy.
- Deterministically group only records whose organisation, facility, patient, local day, and linkage evidence agree.
- Map a legacy state only when its stored timestamps/relations prove one canonical meaning. Ambiguous IN_TRIAGE, TRIAGED, IN_CONSULTATION, COMPLETED, CANCELLED, and Encounter CLOSED cases remain unresolved inventory items.
- A legacy Encounter CLOSED with proven signed ClinicalNote/version may map to SIGNED. Absence of proof does not authorize automatic VOIDED, a fabricated signature, or a MIGRATED reason.
- New writes use canonical states and dual-populate required compatibility fields; reads compare old/new projections during the verification window.
- Activate NOT NULL, partial uniqueness, and canonical CHECK constraints only after zero unresolved affected rows and approved reconciliation evidence.
- Retain rollback as read fallback/feature disable until parity; never destructive-rewrite final history.

MIGRATED_HISTORICAL and MIGRATED_UNRESOLVED are not Product states or reasons and are not introduced.

---

## 14. Laboratory Architecture

laboratory owns LabOrder, independently stateful LabOrderItem, specimen/custody records, result versions, verification/release facts, and correction history. Result values remain unavailable to clinical consumers until verified and RELEASED. Released corrections append a version and preserve prior release, attribution, required review, and supplied notification outcome.

The structured catalogue is versioned as LabTestDefinition -> ordered LabAnalyteDefinition -> zero or more LabReferenceRange rows. Result entry stores each analyte outcome as an immutable/version-owned LabResultAnalyteValue with the applied unit/range snapshot. Catalogue edits never rewrite a released result. Neutral HIGH/LOW flags are only the exact configured-range comparison supplied by LAB-001/LAB-010; no interpretive text or clinical action is generated.

The patient-facing LAB QueueEntry is owned by scheduling and represents collection/receipt interaction only. Under PAY_BEFORE no such entry exists until the item is released from AWAITING_PAYMENT. The LAB QueueEntry completes after SAMPLE_COLLECTED/receipt interaction and is never reopened or completed by result release. Bench lifecycle stays in LabOrderItem.

Readiness is a pure laboratory query over every blocking item plus an idempotent application command that locks the Encounter/Queue and relevant items in canonical order. It transitions readiness only when every blocker is RELEASED or CANCELLED. SAMPLE_REJECTED is non-terminal and remains visible for recollection/cancellation. Partial release exposes released items and progress but triggers neither RESULTS_READY nor READY_TO_RESUME.

LAB_ONLY request capture is not a patient-facing collection QueueEntry. It uses the exact Rank 1 Visit-linked order, billing, payment gate, and later collection-entry behavior. No reception user enters clinical results. No automated critical-value interpretation or external notification is enabled while the relevant authority is unresolved.

---

## 15. Pharmacy and Inventory Architecture

### 15.1 Catalogue and classification boundary

pharmacy owns the minimal medication product identity and authoritative classification seam needed by supplied prescription/dispense refusal. CAT governance and OD-PH classification authority remain blocked. Unclassified or uncertain product authority denies affected protected actions. Controlled/Class A is refused at receiving, prescribing, sale, dispensing, and external-prescription completion in baseline V1; classification changes cannot enable it.

No ClinicalRule or RegulatoryRule table is created merely to preserve a seam. A named provider interface returns an approved versioned rule only when Rank 1 authority supplies it; unavailable authority produces the specified block. The Blueprint never embeds dosing, interaction, allergy matching, diagnosis, risk scoring, critical-value interpretation, or clinical thresholds.

### 15.2 Prescription and provisional Dispense

pharmacy owns both Prescription and Dispense. Draft prescription content is not pharmacy-actionable. The encounter_sign application command activates only the supplied internally dispensable prescription after valid signing; external-only, Pharmacy-disabled, controlled/Class A, and other bounded outcomes follow SM-06. Exactly one source identity prevents duplicate activation/work.

A pharmacist confirms a proposed basket before a stable provisional Dispense is created. The provisional Dispense records proposed lines/source identities but causes no physical handover and no StockMovement. Under PAY_BEFORE, the same Dispense becomes AWAITING_PAYMENT and the same pharmacy QueueEntry is held; release resumes those same records. Cancellation before handover is terminal and produces no stock movement. Partial/not-dispensed outcomes follow Rank 1 and remain explicit.

Opening an active undispensed prescription uses PharmacyClaim. A partial unique constraint permits one unreleased claim per Prescription; the winning row records pharmacist and server time, and another pharmacist receives 409 with the authorised claimant name. No timeout, automatic expiry, supervisor takeover, or general manual release is invented. The active exclusivity ends only with the existing supplied terminal dispense/prescription outcome in the same outer command; the immutable claim retains `released_at` and the terminal source reference. An interrupted claimant may resume their own claim.

Substitution is executable only when the replacement has the same generic identity, strength and form, or an explicit prescriber authorisation is recorded. The immutable substitution evidence records prescribed and dispensed product, basis, reason, whether a prescriber was consulted and who; different strength/form without new prescription is refused. The record/label/receipt projection shows both products, and the prescriber worklist reads the same evidence. No broader equivalence algorithm exists.

DSP-013 OTC sale is a supplied standalone retail path. It may be anonymous or identify an existing Patient, creates a standalone Invoice, Payment, Receipt, Dispense/lines and stock movement bundle in one transaction, and therefore cannot expose paid-without-stock or stock-without-payment. Controlled/Class A is refused. A prescription-only product requires a valid linked Prescription; the OD-PH8/OD-13 exception is not mounted. Manual cash/manual-MoMo references use the ordinary supplied Payment contracts and no provider integration.

### 15.3 Ledger and availability

inventory owns immutable Batch identity, StockLocation, lockable BatchLocationBalance projection, append-only StockMovement, receiving, count, adjustment, transfer, quarantine, and disposal facts. The movement ledger is authoritative. Balance is a transactional projection recomputed/tested against movements and is never directly edited by an API or administrator.

Usable availability excludes expired, quarantined, disposed, recalled where supplied, and otherwise non-usable stock. FEFO orders eligible batches by expiry and stable tie-breaker; it does not bypass a confirmed user basket or substitute product meaning. Final handover rechecks authoritative time, classification, expiry, usable balance, and basket version under locks. One committed handover inserts one source-unique movement bundle and cannot make usable stock negative.

Expired stock has no override, permission, or configuration. It may move only through the supplied quarantine, correction, and disposal paths. Physical returns preserve batch identity and, while OD-PH5 is unresolved, increase only quarantined/non-usable stock. OD-T3 keeps the second-approver exception inactive; no single-user waiver is inferred.

CMD-063 ordinary GRN posting accepts only non-expired lines; any expired line is rejected with `EXPIRED_STOCK_CANNOT_BE_RECEIVED` and creates no ordinary StockReceipt/StockReceiptLine or usable movement. The canonical recovery is a separate CMD-117 non-usable intake: it records the rejected-delivery source, batch/expiry, actor and reason and creates only a quarantine-bucket movement/projection plus QuarantineRecord. It never creates usable quantity or an ordinary posted GRN. The Blueprint deliberately does not decide whole-delivery versus line-level mixed-GRN UX; operators submit eligible GRN lines and non-usable intake as distinct Product actions.

Stock-count submission freezes line evidence and computes variances. Below the configured value threshold, the supplied posting path may create `COUNT_CORRECTION` adjustments immediately; above it, the adjustment remains `PENDING_APPROVAL`. CMD-066 requires a distinct `FACILITY_ADMIN`, rechecks the count watermark and every balance, and atomically posts source-unique movements and immutable count/adjustment evidence. OD-T3's second-approver waiver remains inactive and cannot enable self-approval.

### 15.4 Handover transaction

dispense_handover locks the Dispense, verifies the applicable payment gate, locks balances in stable order, revalidates every line, inserts movements, and transitions Dispense/Prescription in one transaction. Printing is downstream presentation of the committed record; printer failure cannot roll back a handover that already committed, and reprint does not repeat stock or payment effects.

---

## 16. Financial Architecture

### 16.1 Ownership and authoritative calculations

billing owns Invoice, InvoiceLine, Payment, PaymentAllocation, Receipt, CashierShift, CreditNote, Refund, and refundable-credit projection. There is no separate payments Django app.

The sole billing projection implements Rank 1 SM-08 definitions:

- gross Invoice total is the sum of non-voided source lines;
- current authoritative amount due incorporates valid CreditNotes without rewriting gross lines;
- current effective applied value is derived from effective allocations of CONFIRMED, non-reversed Payments;
- outstanding balance, Invoice state, and explicit refundable credit are recomputed from those values;
- cached totals, if retained for reads, are transactional projections checked against the authoritative calculation.

No view, serializer, client, or other domain reimplements these formulas.

### 16.2 Target relations

Payment is an immutable value-received aggregate without an authoritative single-Invoice foreign key. PaymentAllocation is the explicit many-to-many value application. Receipt is a separate retained record linked to Payment and issue/reprint history. Whole-Payment reversal preserves the original Payment, makes every currently effective allocation ineffective through attributable reversal facts, recomputes every affected Invoice independently, and reevaluates only eligible undelivered gates.

CreditNote changes current authoritative amount due without editing paid lines or gross total. A bounded Refund is a separate money-out record against refundable credit; it leaves the original Payment CONFIRMED and cannot alter unrelated allocations, Invoices, or gates. All creditable/refundable caps are checked under lock with source-unique constraints.

### 16.3 Commands

| Command | Locks / atomic bundle | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| invoice_source_line_add | Invoice | exact-one source line; state recomputed |
| payment_confirm | affected Invoices in UUID order; new Payment | one Payment, allocations, Receipt, Invoice projections, gate releases |
| payment_reverse | eligible dependent QueueEntries, Payment, affected Invoices | all allocations reversed, every Invoice recomputed, undelivered-only gate effects |
| credit_note_create | referenced Invoice | one bounded CreditNote, amount due/state/credit recomputed |
| refund_create | Payment when referenced, then Invoice | one bounded Refund, refundable balance reduced, unrelated context unchanged |
| invoice_reprice | PriceList/version, Visit, Invoice | append one complete replacement line-set version, atomically select it and recompute on the same ISSUED Invoice; no line void/payment mutation |
| shift_close | Shift and its immutable payment set | blind declaration then computed variance evidence; no payment edits |

External card, bank, Mobile Money, provider, webhook, or direct-payment integration is not implied. Only manually entered references explicitly supplied by Rank 1 are represented.

REC-003 repricing uses an append-only complete line-set version. Every InvoiceLine carries `line_set_version`; Invoice carries `current_line_set_version`. Under the locked Invoice, CMD-003 validates the wholly unpaid/unpartially-paid precondition, appends every affected eligible replacement line in version `n+1`, then atomically updates the VisitPayerBinding, Invoice pointer and totals while state remains ISSUED. Readers select only the pointed line set. Prior line versions remain immutable history and are neither BIL-004 `VOID` lines nor counted in the current gross total. Failure leaves pointer, payer binding, old lines and totals unchanged; a competing command loses on Invoice version/If-Match and no mixed set is visible.

### 16.4 Staged migration from invoice-local Payment

1. Expand Payment/Allocation/Receipt/reversal fields and add canonical CONFIRMED/VOIDED compatibility values.
2. Backfill one allocation from each valid legacy Payment.invoice relation and verify amount equality and organisation/facility consistency.
3. Build authoritative projection parity for every Invoice; quarantine mismatches for manual financial reconciliation.
4. Dual-read old FK and allocation projection, alert on divergence, and send all new writes through target application commands.
5. Cut client/API reads to allocations only after PostgreSQL concurrency/property gates pass.
6. Make Payment.invoice nullable, retain it as compatibility evidence during rollback window, then remove only in a later contract migration with approved reconciliation record.
7. Rename POSTED to CONFIRMED and VOID to VOIDED only after old/new API compatibility and data counts verify.

No final record is deleted or silently rewritten during migration.

---

## 17. Audit, Privacy, Errors, and API Contract

### 17.1 Transactional audit

Domain services return a small AuditFact containing event code, entity type/ID, safe before/after state names, source IDs, actor/approver IDs, reason code, and authoritative timestamp where required. The application command writes all mandatory facts through audit.services in the same transaction. If required audit insertion fails, the command fails and rolls back.

That same-transaction rule governs successful protected mutations. A Rank-1-required denied-attempt audit uses DENIAL-AUDIT-01 instead:

1. the application command evaluates the action inside its ordinary tenant transaction and produces a typed refusal plus a predeclared redacted DenialAuditFact;
2. the business transaction rolls back completely, including its idempotency claim and any success audit;
3. only after rollback, the application coordinator opens a new short `tenant_atomic` transaction for the same verified organisation and inserts one append-only AuditEvent keyed by organisation, canonical operation, denial request identity and event code;
4. the denial fact contains actor ID, organisation/facility scope, capability/action, safe blocker type and opaque blocker reference, decision code and time; it contains no patient name, clinical content, result value, medication instruction, financial line detail or raw request payload;
5. a duplicate same-fingerprint denial reuses the unique denial event, while materially different request-identity reuse is refused under the ordinary idempotency rule; and
6. the original canonical denial is returned only after the required denial event commits. If the denial-audit transaction fails, the API returns a non-sensitive retryable technical 503 with no invented Product-domain error code, states that no Product mutation committed, and instructs retry with the same request identity; it never reports success or fabricates a committed denial audit.

For DENIAL-AUDIT-01, denial_identity is a canonical operation-scoped request identity derived from the verified organisation, facility scope, actor authority epoch, canonical operation, target opaque IDs and the caller's idempotency key (or the exact supplied denial request identity where Rank 1 names one). denial_fingerprint is SHA-256 over the canonical denial inputs and expected versions; denial_event_code is the stable Rank 1 denial code. PC-047 enforces a partial unique key on organisation plus denial identity. A retry with the same identity, fingerprint and event code returns the already committed denial event; reuse with a materially different fingerprint or event code returns an idempotency conflict. This denial identity is separate from PC-048's successful-command replay record and never commits Product mutation. PHI-minimal safe metadata and audit-write failure behavior remain as stated below.

DENIAL-AUDIT-01 is permitted only for exact Rank 1 requirements such as REC-012-AC06 and the REC-005/REC-009 privileged `PARTIALLY_PAID` refusal. It is not a general mechanism for logging every validation failure. PG-G6 proves business rollback, durable denial evidence, idempotent retry, PHI minimisation and audit-write failure behavior.

AuditEvent remains append-only in application code and PostgreSQL. Updates/deletes are denied by database trigger under the production role. Audit payloads contain no raw clinical note, result value, diagnosis text, patient name/contact, free-text complaint, medication instruction, or other PHI. Reconstruction uses authorized domain records and safe references.

Detailed AUD event catalogue, read/access review, export, and retention remain authority-blocked. Only events explicitly required by supplied stories/TI-13 are enabled. Mounting audit URLs is not permission to invent an audit product surface.

### 17.2 Logs and telemetry

Structured logs allow request ID, operation, code, duration, status, organisation-safe opaque reference where approved, and non-sensitive record UUID. They exclude bodies, query terms, tokens, cookies, Authorization, clinical content, result values, patient identifiers/names, invoice detail, and audit before/after payloads. Error tracking receives the same redacted envelope. Redaction tests fail CI for representative PHI canaries.

### 17.3 Canonical errors

Owner services raise typed domain failures mapped once at the API edge to Rank 1 canonical error family/code, safe message, retry/recovery action, and authorized current-state reference. Scope-hidden records return the Rank 1 non-disclosing result. Database IntegrityError is translated only when the exact named constraint is known; unknown constraint errors remain internal and non-sensitive.

The API does not invent HTTP semantics that alter Rank 1. Authentication/authorization, missing precondition, stale version, duplicate operation, invalid state, prerequisite, and unsupported operation use one documented mapping and generated schema. A safe loser receives the committed current outcome where Rank 1 permits, not a generic success that replays side effects.

### 17.4 REST and OpenAPI

REST endpoints are versioned under /api/v1. Mutating routes represent commands rather than generic CRUD for stateful aggregates. Every command schema documents required Idempotency-Key and/or If-Match, canonical errors, authority scope, and committed result reference. GET ETags are record/projection-version based, not a substitute for mutation-state locks.

OpenAPI is generated from the backend contract. Frontend API types and client functions are generated and checked for drift; handwritten feature view models may wrap generated types but cannot duplicate server enums or request schemas. Schema generation, generated-client compilation, and a clean regeneration diff are CI gates.

---

## 18. Frontend Architecture

### 18.1 Client authority and state

The Next.js client is presentation and interaction only. Patient charts and protected operational pages are client-rendered after authorization; they are not SSR content. Access token, current facility, protected form drafts, and PHI query data remain in memory. Refresh credential remains secure HttpOnly cookie behavior. No PHI, access token, patient chart, clinical draft, result, invoice, stock, or protected query cache is written to localStorage, sessionStorage, IndexedDB, Cache API, service-worker storage, or persisted TanStack cache.

### 18.2 Query identity and facility switching

Every protected query key begins with authority epoch, organisation ID, facility ID, and resource identity. The epoch changes on sign-in, sign-out, refresh authority change, facility switch, or revocation event.

Facility switch sequence:

1. cancel protected in-flight queries;
2. increment authority epoch;
3. clear/remove protected query and mutation state, not merely invalidate it;
4. set the new requested facility context;
5. refetch only after server session/capability confirmation.

Query/mutation responses capture the originating epoch and facility. A late response from an old epoch is discarded and cannot populate the new facility cache. Sign-out performs the same cancellation/clear before rendering public content. This strengthens the current implementation, which clears on sign-out but only invalidates on facility switch.

### 18.3 Feature composition

The current consultations page is decomposed without UX or product changes into feature modules: encounter shell, history, complaints, allergies, examination, diagnoses, treatment, disposition/follow-up, lab panel, prescription panel, and sign/close controls. Each module owns local presentation state and generated API hooks; shared authoritative state lives in server records/query cache.

Loading, empty, stale/conflict, permission-denied, payment-hold, unknown-outcome, and retry states are explicit. Offline/timeouts for high-impact commands say pending/unknown and require server confirmation; they never display completed based only on local state. Low-bandwidth screens use bounded fields, pagination, cancellation, compression, and progressive disclosure without changing authoritative outcomes.

### 18.4 Accessibility and print

Core workflows meet keyboard, focus, accessible-name, error association, contrast, and responsive requirements. Status is conveyed by text as well as color. Modal focus and live-region behavior is tested. Print views render from committed server records, use authorized minimal data, include the supplied provenance, and treat reprint as presentation plus the exact supplied owner mutation/audit, not a repeat of the underlying payment, visit or clinical action. For REC-006, CMD-012 locks the scheduling Visit, computes the next REPRINT (n) marker from PC-006.slip_print_count, increments that counter exactly once only with a successful deterministic render, and records the same count/content hash in DOCUMENT_REPRINTED; a render or audit failure rolls the counter back. Client-local browser printing does not create a second Product action.

RCP-005 printer selection is a frontend-only, non-PHI workstation preference. The browser may persist only `document_class -> local printer/display label` and paper-size choice; the value contains no organisation, facility, user, patient, record or document identifier and grants no authority. No server `PrintConfiguration` aggregate or mutation route exists. Absence or invalidity falls back to the standard browser print dialog. Header/footer content comes from authorised committed source records under RCP-004; RCP-005 never selects clinical/report content or unblocks REP.

---

## 19. Async Work, External Effects, and OD-T1

OD-T1 is OPEN: Rank 1 has not decided whether V1 needs an outbox. Therefore v1.0 does not require or design an Outbox model, outbox skeleton, broker, worker queue, Redis client, or exactly-once external delivery mechanism.

All high-impact V1 commands complete their authoritative database outcome synchronously in the request transaction. Printing consumes the committed outcome. Authorized maintenance/sweep work invokes the same idempotent application commands from a management command or scheduler with explicit tenant context. Work is derived from authoritative rows and safe checkpoints; it is not evidence that an outbox exists.

For a future supplied outbound effect, application defines a DeferredEffectDispatcher protocol and a no-effect/blocking adapter. Selecting synchronous delivery, database outbox, or another durable mechanism is deferred until OD-T1 and the effect's Product authority are resolved. Transactional email behavior remains OPEN/BLOCKED. SMS, WhatsApp, provider payments, webhooks, and other direct integrations remain unauthorized unless Rank 1 changes.

Reporting definitions, exports, delivery, and retention are REP/AUD/legal authority-dependent. The reporting app may expose query interfaces only after a named slice; it does not create export jobs, object-storage requirements, or report content while blocked.

---

## 20. Time, Low Bandwidth, and No-Offline Boundary

PostgreSQL and application timestamps are stored as timezone-aware UTC. The only V1 operational timezone is Africa/Kampala (EAT). Facility-day calculations, Visit uniqueness, expiry comparison, queue dates, schedules, and reports use that fixed IANA zone and the authoritative server/database clock.

If a compatibility Facility.timezone field is introduced, its V1 default and validation/constraint allow exactly Africa/Kampala. It is not exposed as user-configurable product behavior. Client/device time may format a display but never decides expiry, ordering, gate, duration, state, or audit chronology.

Clinical event time entered as domain data remains distinct from system/audit chronology. Tests cover EAT local-day boundaries using real timezone conversion; no naive datetime or server-host local timezone is permitted.

Low-bandwidth support uses small response projections, cursor pagination, request cancellation, compression, retry with the same idempotency key, and explicit unknown outcomes. High-impact stock, money, dispense, final clinical sign, result release, and Visit closure do not complete offline. No service worker queues those commands.

---

## 21. Persistence and Database Invariants

### 21.1 Common fields

Tenant aggregates use UUID primary keys, organisation_id, facility_id where applicable, created_at/updated_at, and an integer version where stale writes matter. State timestamps and actor references are explicit. Foreign keys use PROTECT for final/history-bearing records. Domain records are not soft-deleted as a substitute for supplied terminal/correction states.

### 21.2 Database enforcement

Database constraints back local invariants that are valid independent of workflow:

- organisation/facility consistency and required tenant context;
- canonical state CHECK constraints after migration;
- unique source-event identities for exact-one charges, prescriptions, dispenses, movements, credits, refunds, receipts, and idempotency operations;
- partial uniqueness for supplied active Visit/Queue/Encounter rules;
- nonnegative materialized usable balance;
- positive monetary/quantity records and bounded allocation/refund totals through locked services plus property checks;
- append-only triggers/permissions for audit and, where selected in an approved ADR, stock/final-history tables.

Workflow transitions remain in services; database constraints are defense in depth, not hidden workflows.

### 21.3 Numbering

Human-readable Invoice, Receipt, Encounter, and other supplied references use an owner-specific NumberSequence only if Rank 1 requires a sequence. Allocation occurs under the command transaction and stable lock order. Gaps caused by rollback are acceptable only if product/fiscal authority allows them; fiscal/legal numbering behavior remains blocked under OD-L6. Random current references are implementation evidence, not target authority.

### 21.4 Authoritative projections

BatchLocationBalance and cached invoice totals are rebuildable projections. Each has a reconciliation query comparing it with immutable movements/allocations and a monitor that reports a non-PHI discrepancy reference. Reconciliation never directly edits history; correction uses the supplied domain path.

---

## 22. Migration Strategy

### 22.1 Universal sequence

Every material migration follows:

1. Expand: additive nullable columns/tables/indexes and compatibility readers.
2. Inventory: count/classify every legacy row and produce non-PHI reconciliation evidence.
3. Backfill: deterministic, restartable, tenant-bounded chunks with stable checkpoints.
4. Verify: totals, constraints, source identity, state mapping, RLS, and old/new projection parity.
5. Dual-read; dual-write only for new writes where required and always from one command.
6. Cutover: feature flag/route selects target read and write path after gates pass.
7. Constrain: NOT NULL, CHECK, partial unique, trigger, and RLS after zero unresolved rows.
8. Contract: remove compatibility fields/code only in a later reversible deployment after observation.

Rollback means disable target route and restore compatibility read while retaining new evidence. It never deletes final records, reverses a Product outcome, or restores a schema that cannot represent committed data.

### 22.2 Required migration plans

| Area | Expand/backfill | Verification | Cutover/contract |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Visit | scheduling.Visit plus nullable FKs | grouping and exception inventory; same-day counts | canonical new writes, then constraints; no invented closure |
| Queue | new canonical state column/version | per-row evidence mapping and worklist parity | target worklists; retire legacy states only after zero ambiguous |
| Triage | TriageRecord table/name compatibility and Visit FK | content/version/actor equality | model rename and constraints; no threshold invention |
| Encounter | Visit FK, canonical state, pending/void fields | signed-version proof; ambiguous CLOSED inventory | target services; old state removed later |
| Invoice | VOIDED compatibility, source IDs, projection fields | line/allocation/credit totals per Invoice | target projection authoritative; cache parity monitor |
| Payment | CONFIRMED compatibility, allocation-first links, Receipt/reversal | amount and allocation equality; affected-Invoice tests | nullable then remove invoice FK in later contract |
| New lab/pharmacy/inventory | additive authorized tables only | RLS, state, source uniqueness, ledger/projection property tests | enable per approved slice; no empty behavior stubs |
| New tenant tables | organisation/facility keys and explicit RLS | policy/FORCE/role probe | deployment blocked until proof passes |

### 22.3 Manual exception control

An ambiguous migration row remains on the old read path and blocks constraint/cutover for its affected tenant/slice. Reconciliation requires an authorized reviewer, evidence references, chosen canonical mapping, reason, timestamp, and audit. The tool displays only the minimum authorized data. No agent or migration script chooses clinical, financial, stock, or identity meaning from missing evidence.

---

## 23. Test Strategy and PostgreSQL Proof

### 23.1 Required test layers

Every slice includes:

- owner service tests for legal/illegal transitions and local invariants;
- application-command tests for complete CMC success/failure atomicity;
- API schema, canonical error, permission, and scope tests;
- generated tenant-isolation negative tests for every new tenant endpoint;
- audit assertions including safe payload and rollback on audit failure;
- idempotency same-request replay and different-payload refusal;
- stale-write and single-winner concurrency tests;
- migration/backfill/reconciliation and rollback/correction tests;
- frontend loading, empty, error, conflict, unknown, permission, low-bandwidth, and accessibility tests;
- OpenAPI generation/client type drift checks;
- PHI canary tests for logs, errors, audit, browser storage, and cache clearing.

### 23.2 PostgreSQL gates

| Gate | Proof |
| --- | --- |
| PG-G1 | Every organisation_id table has ENABLE/FORCE RLS and correct USING/WITH CHECK |
| PG-G2 | Application role is non-owner, NOSUPERUSER, NOBYPASSRLS; missing tenant context fails closed |
| PG-G3 | Cross-organisation and unauthorized cross-facility reads/writes fail without disclosure; BRN absent by default |
| PG-G4 | New migrations apply RLS and immutable-history triggers correctly, including rollback path |
| PG-G5 | REC-002 patient registration commits before separately idempotent check-in; check-in failure never rolls registration back; competing Visit/Queue/Encounter/Lab/sign/close transitions have one winner and safe loser |
| PG-G6 | Idempotency first-use race, replay, fingerprint conflict, rollback, lost-response recovery, successful audit rollback, and DENIAL-AUDIT-01 durable-denial/audit-failure behavior |
| PG-G7 | Stock movement append-only, ordered FEFO locks, expiry refusal, and no negative usable balance |
| PG-G8 | Concurrent dispense/count approval/transfer/return, expired non-usable intake and OTC sale produce ledger-projection equality, no duplicate movement and no usable credit from expired stock |
| PG-G9 | Partial/ordinary unique constraints enforce open Visit, active queue path, one Encounter, one active ANC enrolment/contact sequence, one active PharmacyClaim, and every source identity |
| PG-G10 | Under READ COMMITTED, stale If-Match/version cannot overwrite newer clinical/ANC/queue/financial/inventory state; retryable 40001/40P01 failures retry the whole command at most three times with the same key/fingerprint |
| PG-G11 | Tenant-scoped scheduled/query work cannot execute without explicit tenant context; report queries do not leak |
| PG-G12 | Multi-Invoice allocation, whole-Payment reversal, CreditNote, Refund, complete-version Invoice repricing, OTC money/receipt/stock atomicity, and concurrent balance recompute satisfy RG-08 |

All PG gates run against PostgreSQL in CI. SQLite may remain a fast developer test lane but cannot be the required backend gate. CI must add a PostgreSQL service initialized with migration and application roles, run migrations as the migration role, run tests as the application role, and execute check_rls plus direct catalog assertions.

### 23.3 Regression and journey proof

RG-01..RG-15 remain non-waivable. Tests cover the 194 supplied stories through traceable owner/application/API cases, CMC-01..17, and Journeys J-01..J-05 only. Journey F is not reconstructed. ANC tests compose the standard Visit/Encounter/Lab/Prescription paths. A test name includes stable Product IDs and the generated coverage report fails for orphaned supplied IDs.

---

## 24. Deployment, Operations, and Runtime Verification

### 24.1 Bounded target

The artifact is one backend release plus one Next.js client. PostgreSQL is required for any environment making security/concurrency claims. Process count and hosting vendor are operational choices after authority; no production infrastructure is provisioned while OD-L1/OD-L2 remain blocked. Redis in local Compose may be removed or left unused for developer compatibility, but is not a target dependency.

Object storage is not baseline. Authorized print artefacts may stream from server responses or be regenerated from authoritative records. If later supplied attachments/exports require durable blobs, residency, retention, access, and provider authority must resolve first.

### 24.2 Pre-deploy checks

- exact release/version and migration set recorded;
- database backup and tested restore procedure appropriate to the authorized environment;
- migration dry-run, backfill estimates, rollback feature flag, and exception count;
- RLS/role/policy/trigger probes green;
- no unresolved reconciliation rows for enabled constraints;
- generated schema/client clean;
- security/privacy/a11y/PG gates green;
- Africa/Kampala and authoritative clock probe green;
- application starts with Redis/broker/object-store credentials absent;
- no blocked domain route or feature flag is enabled.

Backup retention, region, processor, recovery objectives, incident process, and production monitoring authority remain subject to Rank 1 blockers. The Blueprint requires proof before production but does not invent their policy values.

### 24.3 Runtime monitors

Non-PHI monitors check RLS coverage, idempotency conflict rate, state-transition conflict rate, stuck authorized work, invoice projection parity, stock ledger/balance parity, audit insertion failure, and migration reconciliation count. Alerts carry opaque references only. A monitor detects; it does not auto-correct product state unless Rank 1 supplies that command.

---

The trace generator maps each story and stable AC to state-machine cases, CMCs, TIs/RGs, owner service tests, application-command tests, API tests, frontend tests where applicable, and blocker IDs. Stable AC canonicalization is generated from Rank 1 text/IDs; the Blueprint does not restate ACs as a competing authority.

### 25.2 Architecture gap matrix

| Area | Rank 1 requirement | Current evidence | Target / treatment | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Tenant boundary | Organisation root, Facility branch, fail closed | RLS/tenant helper partial; SQLite default | explicit tenant transaction, PG role/policy gates | PARTIAL -> DEFINED |
| AUTH/TEN/USR | server authority, missing denies | partial session/roles | interfaces and denial only; lifecycle blocked | BLOCKED |
| Patient | identity/merge dependencies | partial Patient/link models | preserve; TRI-013 bounded merge only | PARTIAL/BLOCKED |
| Visit | SM-02 parent | missing | scheduling.Visit, staged reconciliation | MISSING -> DEFINED |
| Queue | SM-01 | noncanonical current states | owner service and safe state migration | CONFLICT -> DEFINED |
| Triage | SM-03, human-entered | TriageAssessment partial | rename/version, no interpretation | PARTIAL -> DEFINED |
| Encounter/history | SM-04, same Encounter, immutable sign | partial sign/version; no lab loop | application coordination; safe legacy reconciliation | PARTIAL -> DEFINED |
| Laboratory | SM-05 and separate queue/bench | empty seam | new owner implementation after authorization | MISSING/BLOCKED |
| Prescription/Dispense | SM-06/07 | empty pharmacy seam | one pharmacy owner; no prescribing app | MISSING/BLOCKED |
| Inventory | append-only, FEFO, expiry/no negative | empty seam | ledger + lockable projection | MISSING/BLOCKED |
| Finance | SM-08/09, multi-Invoice, corrections | invoice-local Payment, partial models | allocation authority and staged decoupling | CONFLICT -> DEFINED |
| Audit | transactional, PHI-minimal, immutable | partial; URL unrouted; PG trigger | safe audit facts; surface/retention blocked | PARTIAL/BLOCKED |
| Idempotency | retry safety/single outcome | org+key, check/save race | operation scope inside outer transaction | CONFLICT -> DEFINED |
| Frontend | no browser authority/persistence | memory token/cache; late facility response gap | authority-epoch cache isolation | PARTIAL -> DEFINED |
| API/types | canonical errors/generated contract | handwritten feature types | OpenAPI generation and drift gate | PARTIAL -> DEFINED |

## 26. Implementation Slice Plan

### 26.1 Status vocabulary

Design status:

- DRAFT: architecture still being reconciled.
- DEFINED: v1.0 contains a coherent testable HOW and, while this document is FROZEN, that HOW is active Rank 2 authority subject to Rank 1.
- BLOCKED: a complete HOW cannot be selected without Product authority.

Implementation authorization:

- AUTHORIZED: an authorized planning source and frozen Blueprint permit the complete slice.
- AUTHORIZED FOR UNAFFECTED BOUNDED WORK: only explicitly named work outside unresolved behavior may proceed.
- BLOCKED: work may not implement the slice now.
- NOT CURRENT RELEASE: outside the current release.

Current v1.0 status is FROZEN with Rank 2 active. Freeze alone grants no blanket slice implementation authorization: each slice remains BLOCKED until explicitly authorized, and every applicable Rank 1 blocker continues to fail closed. S-00 is the next eligible controlled slice, not an implicitly started slice.

### 26.2 Common slice contract

Every slice must include migration expand/backfill/verify/cutover/rollback where data changes; backend owner service and application command; generated REST/OpenAPI contract; frontend loading/empty/error/conflict/unknown/accessibility states; in-transaction safe audit; declared lock and idempotency plan; service/API/permission/tenant/state/audit tests; PostgreSQL gates; runtime verification; and exact Product-ID coverage. A slice cannot exit with a weaker TI/GSC or an enabled blocked branch.

## 29. Governance, Change Control, and Agent Rules

### 29.1 Blueprint change control

A Blueprint change records version, affected BP/ADR/slice/Product IDs, rationale, repository evidence, migration/test impact, authority classification, reviewer, approver, and effective status. A change that alters observable Product behavior is not a Blueprint edit: it stops and follows Rank 1 Product Spec change control.

Stable IDs are never renumbered or silently repurposed. Retired IDs remain in a compact change table with reason and successor. Generated trace and architecture tests reference stable IDs, not section line numbers.

### 29.2 Implementation-agent contract

An implementation agent must:

- read PROJECT_SPEC.md, the then-FROZEN Blueprint, applicable approved ADRs, and AGENTS.md;
- work only on the authorized slice and preserve all blockers;
- inspect current code rather than assume this evidence snapshot is live;
- keep fourteen Django apps and the dependency direction;
- put multi-domain commands in application and local transitions in owner services;
- supply migrations, rollback/correction, tests, PostgreSQL proof, audit, accessibility, generated API validation, and exact results;
- stop when Product authority is missing or current code conflicts with a final record;
- never stage, commit, push, merge, deploy, or open a PR without explicit request.

Completion reports list files changed, decisions, architecture rules, exact validations/results, remaining risks/open decisions, whether canonical architecture changed, current git status, and external actions.

### 29.3 Frozen versus live state

When frozen, this document defines target HOW. Current code, feature flags, migrations, live data, and runtime verification remain live execution state and may lag. Live state never changes Rank 1 or the frozen target. A slice is enabled only after its authorization, migration, data reconciliation, tests, PG gates, and runtime probes pass.

## 31. Target Persistence Contracts

These contracts are normative Rank 2 HOW under v1.0 FROZEN. Field notation is name:type:NN-or-NULL:semantic role. UUID means UUIDv7-compatible UUID. money means Decimal(14,2) in the Organisation currency. qty means a fixed-precision decimal sufficient for the approved unit model. All tenant foreign keys carry the same organisation; facility-derived rows also carry the same facility. NN means non-null. A named state uses only Rank 1 values. OWNER SERVICE is the sole state-transition owner.

Invariant classification codes: DB = DATABASE-ENFORCED; SVC = SERVICE-ENFORCED; DB+SVC = both; CMD = APPLICATION-COMMAND-ENFORCED; AUTH = EXTERNAL/PRODUCT AUTHORITY BLOCKED.

### 31.2 Constraint, correction, RLS, index, and migration contracts

All foreign keys use PROTECT/RESTRICT unless this table explicitly says SET NULL. Correctness indexes back uniqueness, foreign keys used for tenant joins, and lock acquisition. Performance indexes are non-normative for behavior and may change after measurement.
